\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}})\triangleq\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}\,.

We will assemble an estimate for the span of the covariances out of the top-
k
singular subspaces of empirical estimates of
\E_{\mathcal{M}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))],\E_{\mathcal{M}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))],\E_{\mathcal{M}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]
.

For any
i\in[k]
and
s\in\{00,01,11\}
, note that

\displaystyle\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]

\displaystyle=\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{i}]]+\sum_{j\in S_{\rm close}[i]\backslash i}\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j})\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum_{j\in S_{\rm far}[i]}\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j})\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]

\displaystyle=\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]+\sum_{j\in S_{\rm close}[i]\backslash i}\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}))\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{j\in S_{\rm far}[i]}\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}))\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]\,,

(12)

where we used that
\mathcal{K}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{K}_{m}
forms a partition of
\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
.

Constructing an approximation for
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})^{\intercal}
.

We will now argue that the two sums in Eq. (
12
) are negligible compared to the term
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]
. This will allow us to construct a matrix that is close to
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})^{\intercal}
.

In the expression
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]
above, we are recentering
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
around
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})
. We first show that the probability that a sample from the
i
-th component lands in
\mathcal{K}_{j}
for some
j\in S_{\rm far}[i]
is small, meaning that with high probability we are correctly recentering
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
around
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j}
for some
j\in S_{\rm close}[i]
.

Lemma 4.6
.

For any
i\in[k]
,

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}\ \text{for some}\ j\in S_{\rm far}[i]]\leq 1/R^{8}
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\leq k\beta
and
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}=\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\geq k\alpha^{2}
.
Therefore, for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
, we may apply Hanson-Wright (
Fact

4.7
to control the tails of
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\|^{2}
).

Fact 4.7

(Hanson-Wright)
.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfies
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|^{2}_{F}/\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|^{2}_{\rm op}\geq r
. Then for any
s>0
,

\displaystyle\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}})>s\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\Omega(\min(s\sqrt{r},s^{2}))

(13)

\displaystyle\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}})<-s\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\Omega(\min(s\sqrt{r},s^{2}))\,.

(14)

By taking
r
in
Fact

4.7
to be
1
, we find that there is an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\Pr\bigl[\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\|^{2}>k\beta+C^{\prime}\alpha\sqrt{k}\log R\bigr]\leq 1/R^{8}\,.

Given
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
. Thus, conditioned on the above event,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|\leq\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}+\sqrt{k\beta}+k^{1/4}\sqrt{C^{\prime}\alpha\log R}\,.

For any
j\in[k]
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
. If
j\in S_{\rm far}[i]
, we have

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|-3\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}\geq\Delta-3\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}\,.

Thus, conditioned on the above event,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|\geq\Delta-3\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}-\sqrt{k\beta}-k^{1/4}\sqrt{C^{\prime}\alpha\log R}\,.

By our choice of
\Delta
in Eq. (
9
), if
C
therein is a sufficiently large constant, the above is larger than
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|
as desired.
∎

Next, we argue that the “signal terms”
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]
in Eq. (
12
) are well-approximated by the rank-one matrices
\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})^{\intercal}
,
\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}
, and
\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}\,.

Proof.

We will be bounding the operator norm of matrices of
the form of
\E[\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal})]
where
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
is a Gaussian vector.
To do so we take any test vector
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
for which
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}=1
; we will regard it interchangeably as a vector or as a
d\times d
matrix.
We then bound
\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}})^{\intercal}\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}[\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal})]\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}})

using the following simple lemma (that follows from Wicks’ identity for the fourth Gaussian moments).

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be any matrix
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
be a covariance matrix. Then for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})^{2}]=

\displaystyle\;\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\rangle^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}}\|^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\|^{2}+(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})^{2}

\displaystyle+2\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\cdot\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\rangle+2\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}).

Moreover, if
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}\leq 1
and

\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\|_{\rm op}\leq\beta
, then

\Bigl|\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})^{2}]-\;\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\rangle^{2}-2(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\rangle-(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})^{2}\Bigr|\lesssim\max(\beta^{2},\beta\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\|^{2},\beta\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\|^{2})\,.

(15)

Proof.

Writing
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})
as
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle[((\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}))^{2}]=

\displaystyle\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}})^{2}]+\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}})^{2}]+\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})^{2}]+\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})^{2}]

\displaystyle+2\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}})]+2\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\sim\mathcal{N}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}})].

Using the definition of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}
, we have

\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}})^{2}]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}_{i,i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}_{j,j}\E[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}_{i}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}_{j}^{2}]+2\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}_{i,j}^{2}\E[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}_{i}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}_{j}^{2}]=\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}})^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{F}^{2}.

Using the fact that
\E[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{M}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}]=\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{M}})
for any matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{M}}
, we obtain the result.
∎

For the first claimed inequality, we apply Eq. (
15
) from
Lemma

4.9
to
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}\sim{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})
to get

\Bigl|\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\rangle^{2}\Bigr|\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}\,.

Note that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
and
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
, so

\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\rangle^{2}\pm O(\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean})\,.

Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}=(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}
, so because the above bound holds for all
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}
for which
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}=1
, the first claimed inequality follows.

The proof of the second inequality proceeds similarly. By Eq. (
15
) applied to
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}\sim{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})
, we get

\Bigl|\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\rangle^{2}\Bigr|\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}\,.

Note that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
and
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
, so

\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\rangle^{2}\pm O(\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean})\,.

Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}=(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}
, so because the above bound holds for all
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}
for which
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}=1
, the second claimed inequality follows.

For the third inequality, by Eq. (
15
) applied to
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}\sim{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})
, we get

\Bigl|\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\rangle^{2}\Bigr|\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}+\beta\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}\,.

Note that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
and
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
,
so

\E[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}]=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\rangle^{2}\pm O(\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean})\,.

Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}=(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime})^{2}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}
, so because the above bound holds for all
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}
for which
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}=1
, the third claimed inequality follows.
∎

Now if we can show that the remaining terms in Eq. (
12
) have small norm, then we can argue that we can read off a rough approximation of
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})\mathrm{vec}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})^{\intercal}
from
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]
. In the following Lemma, we show the remaining terms in Eq. (
12
) are indeed bounded:

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}
be any vectors from among
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}
. Suppose that either of the following holds:

•

j\in S_{\rm far}[i]
, or

•

j\in S_{\rm close}[i]
and additionally
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}
are centers of components in
S_{\rm close}[i]
.

Then

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}k^{2}+\Delta^{4}

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{3/2}\Delta+\beta\Delta^{2}

\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]=0\,.

Proof.

For
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, define
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\triangleq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}
,
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}\triangleq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}
, and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}\triangleq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
so that
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
be a test vector which we regard interchangeably as a vector and as a
d\times d
matrix, and which satisfies
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\|_{F}=1
.

Proof for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}
: We have

\bigl|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\bigr|=\bigl|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{2}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr|\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\,.

To bound the expectation of this over
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, it suffices to bound
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{2}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]
and
\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime})^{2}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]
. These can be handled in the same way, so here we consider the former.

First suppose that
j\in S_{\rm far}[i]
. By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{2}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]\leq\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{4}]^{1/2}\cdot\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\,.

Note that

\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{4}]^{1/2}\leq\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\|^{8}]^{1/2}\lesssim\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})}[\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\|^{8}]^{1/2}+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})\|^{4}\lesssim\beta^{2}k^{2}+R^{4}\,.

The proof of the first part of the Lemma then follows by the fact that
\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\leq 1/R^{4}
by
Lemma

4.6
, so we get an overall bound of
\beta^{2}k^{2}/R^{4}+1\leq\beta^{2}k^{2}+\Delta^{4}
(as
\Delta,R\geq 1
by assumption).

Next, suppose that
j\in S_{\rm close}[i]
and additionally
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}
are centers of components in
S_{\rm close}[i]
. Then

\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{2}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]]\leq\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}})^{2}]\leq\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\|^{4}]\lesssim\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})}[\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\|^{4}]+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})\|^{4}\lesssim\beta^{2}k^{2}+\Delta^{4}\,,

thus establishing the third part of the Lemma.

Proof for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}
: We have

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]=\bigl((\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}\bigr)\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\,.

Note that the event that
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}
only depends on
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}
, so the expectation of the above over
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
is given by

\displaystyle\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}\bigl[\bigl((\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}\bigr)\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\bigr]

\displaystyle=\E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}}\bigl[\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\cdot\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}}[(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}]\bigr]

\displaystyle=\E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}}\bigl[\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]\cdot\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\rangle\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\cdot\E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}}[\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\intercal}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\cdot\beta\E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\prime}}}[\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}}^{\prime\intercal}\|_{F}^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\cdot\beta\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})}[\|(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}))(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}))^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))^{\intercal}\|_{F}^{2}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\cdot\beta\E_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}[\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime})^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\|^{2}_{F}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Pr[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]^{1/2}\cdot\beta\bigl(\beta^{1/2}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}\|+\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}\|^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}\|^{2}\bigr)\,,

(16)

where in the second step we used that the covariance of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}
is
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}
.

Suppose that
j\in S_{\rm far}[i]
. Then by
Lemma

4.6
, the above can be upper bounded by
\beta^{3/2}/R^{3}+\beta/R^{2}\leq\beta^{3/2}\Delta+\beta\Delta^{2}
(as
\Delta,R\geq 1
by assumption), completing the proof of the second part of the Lemma.

Next, suppose that
j\in S_{\rm close}[i]
and additionally
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}
are centers of components in
S_{\rm close}[i]
. Then Eq. (
16
) can be upper bounded by
\beta^{3/2}\Delta+\beta\Delta^{2}
, completing the proof of the fourth part of the Lemma.

Proof for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}
: We have

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}^{\intercal}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}^{\prime}))\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]=\bigl(({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}-({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}^{\perp})^{2}\bigr)\cdot\mathds{1}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mathcal{K}_{j}]=0\,.

As this holds for all
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{A}}
, the last part of the Lemma follows.
∎

By combining Eq. (
12
) with
Lemma

4.8
and
Lemma

4.10
, we conclude the following:

Corollary 4.11
.

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}+\beta^{2}k^{3}+k\Delta^{4}

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{01}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}+k\beta^{3/2}\Delta+k\beta\Delta^{2}

\|\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{11}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]-\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}\,.

Using
Corollary

4.11
and
Lemma

4.3
, we are now ready to state our algorithm and prove the main guarantee of this section.

Algorithm 3

CrudeEstimateCovariances(
q,\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}\}
)

Input:

Sample access to
q
, estimates
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}

Output:

List
\mathcal{W}
containing approximations to
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{k}

1

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\leftarrow
span of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}

2

Define the functions
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}
from Eq. (
11
) and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\cdot)
from Eq. (
10
) using
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}
.

3

Initialize
\mathcal{W}
to the empty set.

4

Draw samples
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}_{N}
from
q
for
N\leftarrow\mathrm{poly}(dR/\beta)
.

5

for

s\in\{00,01,11\}

do

6

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{s}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum^{N}_{j=1}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}_{j}))

7

\widehat{V}_{s}\leftarrow
top-
k
singular subspace of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{s}

8

\mathcal{W}_{s}\leftarrow
a
\beta
-net over vectors in
\widehat{V}_{s}
with
L_{2}
norm at most
\beta\sqrt{d}

9

end for

10

for

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}\in\mathcal{W}_{00}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}\in\mathcal{W}_{01}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}\in\mathcal{W}_{11}

do

11

Add
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01})^{\intercal}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}
to
\mathcal{W}
.

12

end for

13

return

\mathcal{W}

Proof of
Lemma

4.5
.

Consider the matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}_{00}\triangleq\E_{\mathcal{M}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\E_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Psi}}_{00}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))]
. By standard matrix concentration, for
N=\mathrm{poly}(dR/\beta)
given in
Algorithm

3
, we have that the matrix
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{00}
constructed in Step
Algorithm

3
of
Algorithm

3
satisfies
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{00}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}_{00}\|_{\rm op}\leq\beta
. Therefore, by triangle inequality and
Corollary

4.11
,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{00}-\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\mathrm{vec}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})^{\intercal}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}+\beta^{2}k^{3}+k\Delta^{4}\,.

By
Lemma

4.3
, this means that the top-
k
singular subspace of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{00}
contains
d^{2}
-dimensional vectors
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}_{k}
which, regarded as
d\times d
matrices, satisfy

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\|^{2}_{F}\lesssim\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}+\beta^{2}k^{3}+k\Delta^{4}

for all
i\in[k]
.

In an entirely analogous fashion, we can show that the top-
k
singular subspace of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{01}
contains
d^{2}
-dimensional vectors
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}_{k}
satisfying

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\|^{2}_{F}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}+k\beta^{3/2}\Delta+k\beta\Delta^{2}

Likewise, the top-
k
singular subspace of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{C}}}_{11}
contains
d^{2}
-dimensional vectors
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}_{k}
satisfying

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\|^{2}_{F}\lesssim\beta^{2}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm mean}\,.

Finally, note that

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\|_{F},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\zeta}}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\|_{F},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}^{\perp}_{i})^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}^{\perp}\|_{F}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}\,.

Combining all of these bounds we find that

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}_{i}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}_{i}+(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01})^{\intercal}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\lesssim\beta^{1/2}\upsilon_{\rm mean}^{1/2}+k^{3/2}(\beta+\Delta^{2})\lesssim\beta^{1/2}\upsilon_{\rm mean}^{1/2}+k^{3/2}\upsilon_{\rm mean}+k^{5/2}\beta+k^{2}\alpha\log R\,.

The claim then follows from the fact that
\mathcal{W}_{00},\mathcal{W}_{01},\mathcal{W}_{11}
in Step
Algorithm

3
contain approximations to
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{00}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{01}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{11}_{i}
that are
\beta
-close in operator norm. Finally, note that the size of
\mathcal{W}
is bounded by
d^{O(k)}
, by standard bounds on epsilon-nets.
∎

4.3
Putting everything together

It is straightforward to combine the results of the previous two sections to derive the proof of
Lemma

4.1
. First, for completeness, we provide the pseudocode for the algorithm:

Algorithm 4

CrudeEstimate(
q
)

Input:

Sample access to
q

Output:

List
\mathcal{W}
containing approximations to
(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{1}),\ldots,(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{k},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{k})

1

\mathcal{W}\leftarrow\emptyset

2

\mathcal{W}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}\leftarrow
CrudeEstimateMeans(
q
)

3

for

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}

do

4

\mathcal{W}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}\leftarrow
CrudeEstimateCovariances(
q,\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}\}
)

5

for

i\in[k]
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\in\mathcal{W}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}

do

6

Insert
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}})
into
\mathcal{W}

7

end for

8

end for

9

return

\mathcal{W}

Proof of
Lemma

4.1
.

By
Lemma

4.2
, in some iteration of Line
Algorithm

4
of
Algorithm

4
, we get
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}
which satisfy
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
for
\upsilon_{\rm mean}=O(\beta/\lambda_{\rm min})
. Substituting this into
Lemma

4.5
, we conclude that for each
i\in[k]
, in some iteration of Line
Algorithm

4
of
Algorithm

4
, we get
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfying
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\lesssim k^{3/2}\beta/\lambda_{\rm min}+k^{2}\alpha\log R
, where we used that
\lambda_{\rm min}\leq 1/k
to simplify the bound in
Lemma

4.5
.

For the bound on
|\mathcal{W}|
, note that there are
(R/\sqrt{\beta})^{O(k^{2})}
iterations of the outer loop, within each of which there are
d^{O(k)}
iterations of the inner loop, so
|\mathcal{W}|=(R/\sqrt{\beta})^{O(k^{2})}\cdot d^{O(k)}
as claimed. For the runtime, CrudeEstimateMeans is called exactly once, and CrudeEstimateCovariances is called
(R/\sqrt{\beta})^{O(k^{2})}
times, so the overall runtime of the algorithm is
(R/\sqrt{\beta})^{O(k^{2})}\cdot(\mathrm{poly}(d,1/\beta)+d^{O(k)})
.
∎

5
Clustering via likelihood ratio estimates

In this section we present our main clustering guarantee, which leverages the estimates for the parameters we obtained from the previous section. As those estimates are only crude approximations to the true parameters, we will obtain a commensurately crude clustering. First, we formalize the notion of “clusters” and what it means to give an accurate clustering:

Definition 5.1
.

Let
{\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\}
and
{\cal T}=\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\}
be partitions of
[k]
.

({\cal S},{\cal T})
is a
(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})
-separated partition pair if:

•

For all
a\in[m]
and
i,i^{\prime}\in S_{a}
, we have that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}\|\leq\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm in}
.

•

For all distinct
a,a^{\prime}\in[m]
and
i\in S_{a},i^{\prime}\in S_{a^{\prime}}
, we have that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}\|\geq\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}
.

•

For all
b\in[n]
and
i,i^{\prime}\in T_{b}
, we have that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{F}\leq\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm in}
.

•

For all distinct
b,b^{\prime}\in[n]
and
i\in T_{b},i^{\prime}\in T_{b^{\prime}}
, we have that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{F}\geq\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}
.

Roughly speaking,
{\cal S}
(resp.
{\cal T}
) partitions the mixture components into groups such that any two components in the same group have means (resp. covariances) that are not far, and any two components from two different groups have means (resp. covariances) that are not close. Their common refinement is a partition
{\cal U}
such that any two components in the same group have both means and covariances not too far, and any two components from two different groups either have means not too close or covariances not too close.

By brute-forcing over pairs of partitions of
[k]
(of which there are at most
k^{2k}
), we may assume we have access to
{\cal S}
and
{\cal T}
, and thus to
{\cal U}
. Our goal is then to assign to every
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
an index into the partition
{\cal U}
. For
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
sampled from the
i
-th component of the mixture which belongs to the
t
-th group in
{\cal U}
, we would like our assignment to be
t
with high probability. The main result of this section is to show that this is indeed possible:

Proposition 5.2
.

Suppose
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{k}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfy
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\upsilon_{\rm mean}
and
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{i}\|_{F}\leq\upsilon_{\rm cov}
.

Let
({\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\},{\cal T}=\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\})
denote a
(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})
-separated partition of
[k]
, where

\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\geq\max(5(\beta/\alpha)^{3}\upsilon_{\rm cov},c\alpha)\,,\qquad\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}\geq\max(6\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}},6\sqrt{\beta k})\,,\qquad\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}+\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm in}\leq c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\sqrt{\alpha}/\beta\,.

(17)

for sufficiently small constant
c>0
. Let
\{U_{1},\ldots,U_{{n_{\rm c}}}\}
denote the common refinement of
{\cal S}
and
{\cal T}
.

Then there is an explicit deterministic function
\mathsf{c}:\mdmathbb{R}^{d}\to[{n_{\rm c}}]
using
{\cal S}
,
{\cal T}
, and
\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{i}\}
, such that for any
t\in[{n_{\rm c}}]
and
i\in U_{t}
,

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\mathsf{c}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})\neq t]\leq k^{3}\exp\Bigl(-\Omega\Bigl(\frac{(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out})^{2}}{\alpha\sqrt{k}}\wedge\frac{\alpha^{6}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}}{\beta^{6}\upsilon_{\rm cov}^{2}}\wedge\frac{\alpha^{2}\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}}{\beta^{3}}\Bigr)\Bigr)

At a high level, the idea is as follows. It is not too hard to determine which group in
{\cal S}
a given point
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
should belong to, simply by checking which mean estimate
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}
is closest to
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
after projecting to the subspace spanned by
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}
. For each group in
{\cal S}
, we can then effectively restrict our attention to components within that group and focus on clustering them according to their covariances. Roughly speaking, we accomplish this by comparing log-likelihoods of sampling
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
under
{\cal N}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{1}),\ldots,{\cal N}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{k})
and choosing the group in
{\cal T}
containing the component maximizing log-likelihood.

5.1
Proof preliminaries

First, we need the following basic lemma which implies that given estimates
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{k}
for the covariances of the components, we can produce estimates
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{k}
for the
inverse
covariances:

Lemma 5.3
.

If
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is a psd matrix satisfying
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\|_{F}\leq\upsilon_{\rm cov}
, and
\alpha\,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}}\preceq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\preceq\beta\,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}}
, then
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime-1}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}\|_{F}\leq 4\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d\times d}
defined as follows. Let
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}
have singular value decomposition
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{U}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{U}}^{\intercal}
, and define
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{U}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{U}}^{\intercal}
, where
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}^{\prime}
is given by replacing every diagonal entry of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}
less than
\alpha/2
with
\alpha/2
.

Proof.

Note that there are at most
4\upsilon_{\rm cov}^{2}/\alpha^{2}
diagonal entries of
\Lambda
less than
\alpha/2
, or else we would violate the assumption that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\|_{F}\leq\upsilon_{\rm cov}
. So
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}\|_{F}\leq\upsilon_{\rm cov}
and thus
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}\|_{F}\leq 2\upsilon_{\rm cov}
. Finally, note that
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime-1}\|_{\rm op}=\sigma_{\min}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime})^{-1}\leq 2/\alpha
. We have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime-1}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime-1}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}\|_{F}\leq 4\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}\,.\qed

(18)

Given
i,j\in[k]
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
, define

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})=(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\,.

Note that for any
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}
,

\E_{x\sim{\cal N}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(x;\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})]=\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\,.

Provided
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}
are close, if
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}
then this quantity is close to zero, but if
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}
then this quantity scales as

\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\approx\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j}\rangle=\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j})-2d\,,

which can be quite large in comparison. Motivated by this, we will use
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
to cluster the samples according to the covariances of the components generating them.

5.2
Properties of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\geq 5(\beta/\alpha)^{3}\upsilon_{\rm cov}
. Let
i,j\in[k]
. Suppose
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}\|\leq c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\sqrt{\alpha}/\beta

(19)

for some
c>0
.

If
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\geq\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}
, then for any
c^{\prime}>0
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-\Omega(c^{\prime 2}(\alpha^{4}/\beta^{6})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\min(1,\alpha^{2}/\upsilon_{\rm cov}^{2})))
over
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{j}
,

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})\geq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j}\rangle-E\,,

where

E\triangleq(c^{2}+2c^{\prime})\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}/\beta^{2}+(4\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\,.

Proof.

Define
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}^{1/2}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
. Then for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{j}
, writing this as
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
, we see that the quantity
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})
is distributed as

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}-2\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle+\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\,.

(20)

Controlling
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
: We would like to apply
Fact

4.7
. Note that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\geq\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}\|_{F}-4\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{-1}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{j}-{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}_{i}){\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}}^{-1}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}\|_{F}-4\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(\alpha/\beta^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}-4\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}\gtrsim(\alpha/\beta^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\,,

where in the last step we used the fact that
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}
satisfies
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\geq 5(\beta/\alpha)^{3}\upsilon_{\rm cov}
by hypothesis. Furthermore,
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim(\beta/\alpha)\cdot(\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha+1)
, so

\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{F}/\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{\rm op}\gtrsim(\alpha^{2}/\beta^{3})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\cdot\min(1,\alpha/\upsilon_{\rm cov})
.

Additionally,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{j}\|_{F}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}+\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\,,

where in the last step we used the assumption that
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\geq\upsilon_{\rm cov}
.

By
Fact

4.7
, for any
s>0
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}-

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}))\leq-s(\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\Omega(\min(s(\alpha^{2}/\beta^{3})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\cdot\min(1,\alpha/\upsilon_{\rm cov}),s^{2})))\,.

(21)

We will take

s=c^{\prime}(\alpha^{2}/\beta^{3})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\cdot\min(1,\alpha/\upsilon_{\rm cov})

for arbitrarily small constant
c^{\prime}>0
. By this choice of
s
, we have
s(\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\leq c^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{d}F/\beta^{2}
. Additionally,
s^{2}
is the dominant term in the exponent in Eq. (
21
). Summarizing,

\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}))\leq-c^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}/\beta^{2}\Bigr]\leq\exp(-\Omega(s^{2}))\,.

(22)

Controlling
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle
: Note that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim 1/\alpha
, so
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\|\lesssim\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}/\sqrt{\alpha\beta}
by Eq. (
19
). Note that because
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\gtrsim\beta\geq\alpha^{5/2}/\beta^{3/2}
, we have that
s\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}/\sqrt{\alpha\beta}\leq c^{\prime}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}/\beta^{2}\leq c^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}/\beta^{2}
. By standard Gaussian tail bounds, we conclude that

\Pr[|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle|\geq c^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}/\beta^{2}]\leq\exp(-\Omega(s^{2}))\,.

(23)

Controlling
\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle
: As
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim 1/\alpha
, by Eq. (
19
) we have that

|\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle|\leq c^{2}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}/\beta^{2}\,.

(24)

Putting things together
:
Conditioned on the events of Eq. (
22
) and (
23
) not holding, and also using the bound on the constant term in Eq. (
24
), we see from the decomposition of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
in Eq. (
20
) that

\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{j}}\Bigl[\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\leq-(c^{2}+2c^{\prime})\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}/\beta^{2}\Bigr]\lesssim\exp(-\Omega(s^{2}))\,.

(25)

It remains to bound
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle
. We have

\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\geq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j}\rangle-(4\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2})\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\,.

(26)

Combining this with Eq. (
25
),
we obtain the desired bound.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
i\in[k]
. As in
Lemma

5.4
, suppose
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|\leq c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\sqrt{\alpha}/\beta

(27)

for sufficiently small absolute constant
c>0
.

For any
s\geq 1
,
with probability at least
1-O(k)\cdot\exp(-\Omega(s))
over
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, we have that for all
j\in[k]
,

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})\leq(s\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\{\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\vee\upsilon_{\rm cov}\}+c^{2}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}/\beta^{2}+c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\sqrt{s/\alpha\beta}\,.

Proof.

Define
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
(note these are slightly different from
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}
defined in
Lemma

5.4
as
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}
is sampled from
\mathcal{N}_{i}
instead of
\mathcal{N}_{j}
). Then for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, writing this as
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
, we see that the quantity
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
is distributed as

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}-2\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle+\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle\,.

(28)

Controlling
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
: Note that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i})\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{-1}_{j}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\|_{F}+4\beta\upsilon_{\rm cov}/\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\{\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\vee\upsilon_{\rm cov}\}

By
Fact

4.7
, we have

\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})}\Bigl[|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}^{\intercal}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{B}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}-\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}))|\leq(s\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\{\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\vee\upsilon_{\rm cov}\}\Bigr]\geq 1-2\exp(-\Omega(s))\,.

(29)

Controlling
|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle|
: Note that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim 1/\alpha
, so
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\|\leq c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}/\sqrt{\alpha\beta}
by Eq. (
27
). By standard Gaussian tail bounds, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\exp(-\Omega(s))
,

|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{w}}\rangle|\leq c\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}\sqrt{s/\alpha\beta}\,.

(30)

Controlling
\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle
: As
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim 1/\alpha
, by Eq. (
27
) we have that

|\langle(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})^{\intercal},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle|\leq c^{2}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}/\beta^{2}\,.

(31)

Putting things together
: Conditioned on the events of Eq. (
29
) and (
30
) holding, and also using the bound on the constant term in Eq. (
31
), we see from the decomposition of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})
that

\Pr_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}}\Bigl[|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}})|>(s\beta/\alpha^{2})\cdot\{\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|_{F}\vee\upsilon_{\rm cov}\}+c^{2}(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})^{2}/\beta^{2}+c\sqrt{s/\alpha\beta}\Bigr]\lesssim\exp(-\Omega(s))\,.

The claimed bound follows by a union bound.
∎

5.3
Formally defining the clustering

We are now ready to define our clustering function.

Let
({\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\},{\cal T}=\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\})
denote a
(\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm in},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out},\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out})
-separated partition of
[k]
. First, define

\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})\triangleq a\in[m]\ \text{for which}\ \argmin_{i\in[k]}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|\in S_{a}\,,

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}
is the projector to the span of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{k}
.

The following is a slight modification of
Lemma

4.6
:

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose that
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}\geq\max(6\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}},6\sqrt{k\beta})
. Then
for any
i\in S_{a}
and
a^{\prime}\neq a
,

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})=a^{\prime}]\leq\exp\Bigl(-\Omega\Bigl(\frac{1}{\alpha\sqrt{k}}\min_{i^{\prime}\in S_{a^{\prime}}}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}\|^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr)\,.

Equivalently,

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})\in\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}:i^{\prime}\in S_{a^{\prime}}\}]\leq\exp\Bigl(-\Omega\Bigl(\frac{1}{\alpha\sqrt{k}}\min_{i^{\prime}\in S_{a^{\prime}}}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}\|^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr)\,.

Proof.

Note that
\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}})\leq k\beta
and
\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}\geq k\alpha^{2}
, so for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
, by
Fact

4.7
with
r
therein taken to be
1
, for all
s>0
we have

\Pr[\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\|^{2}>k\beta+s\alpha\sqrt{k}]\leq\exp(-\Omega(s))\,.

Given
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
. Thus, conditioned on the above event,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|\leq\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}+\sqrt{k\beta}+k^{1/4}\sqrt{\alpha s}\,.

Next, for any
i^{\prime}\not\in S_{a}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{i}
, note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}^{1/2}_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}
for
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{z}}\sim{\cal N}(0,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Id}})
. We have

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i})+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i})\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|-3\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|\,,

where in the last step we used that
\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}\geq 6\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}
. Thus, conditioned on the above event,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|-\sqrt{k\beta}-k^{1/4}\sqrt{\alpha s}\,.

Provided that
s>(\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|-\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}-\sqrt{k\beta})^{2}/\alpha\sqrt{k}
, we have that
\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|<\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}_{i^{\prime}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Pi}}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}\|
. As
\sqrt{\upsilon_{\rm mean}}\leq\frac{1}{6}\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}
and
\sqrt{k\beta}\leq\frac{1}{6}\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}_{\rm out}
, it suffices to take
s=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}_{i}\|^{2}}{36\alpha\sqrt{k}}
.

The second part of the Lemma follows by definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})
.
∎

Define
\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})
as follows. First note that we can’t directly use
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}
as it has a term
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle
which depends on the true covariance
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}
. Likewise, the lower and upper bounds on
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ii}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\Lambda}}_{ij}
in
Lemma

5.4
and
Lemma

5.5
depend on the true covariances
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}
.

Instead, we will brute force over guesses for these quantities. Henceforth, suppose we have access to numbers
\{t_{ij}\}
satisfying

\bigl|t_{ij}-(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}^{-1}\rangle-E)\bigr|\leq\eta

for sufficiently small parameter
\eta
, where
E
is the error term from
Lemma

5.4
. Because

|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}^{-1}\rangle-E|\lesssim\beta d/\alpha+\upsilon_{\rm cov}\beta\sqrt{d}/\alpha^{2}\lesssim\beta d/\alpha\,,

we can produce these numbers by brute-forcing over a grid of size
(\beta d/\alpha\eta)^{O(k^{2})}
. We will eventually take

\eta=\frac{\Delta^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}_{\rm out}}{100\beta^{2}}\,.

(32)

With these
\{t_{ij}\}
in hand, given an index
\ell\in[n]
into the partition
\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\}
, we define
\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})=b
if there exists some
i\in T_{b}
such that

(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))^{\intercal}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{i}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{K}}}_{j})(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}}))<t_{ij}-\eta

for all
j\not\in T_{b}
.
If there exist multiple such
b
for which this is the case, then choose one arbitrarily. If no such
b
exists, then set
\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})
to be
0
.

Corollary 5.7
.

For any
i\in S_{a}\cap T_{b}
and nonzero
b^{\prime}\neq b
, we have that

\Pr_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}[\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})=b^{\prime}\mid\mathsf{c}^{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{\mu}})}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{x}})=a]\leq 2k^{2}\exp(-\Omega(c^{\prime 2}(\alpha^{4}/\beta^{6})\cdot\min_{j\in T_{b^{\prime}}}\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{Q}}_{i}\|^{2}_{F}\cdot\min(1,\alpha^{2}/\upsilon_{\rm cov}^{2})))\,.

Proof.

We can rewrite the conditional probability as
